### Arc DYN — rigid-body dynamics + the portfolio dogfood (ACTIVE, resequenced pre-freeze 2026-08-14)

Goal: make the portfolio's page-collapse showcase actually run engine code. **Full design in the
SoT: [dynamics-lift-2026-08.md](dynamics-lift-2026-08.md)**; task breakdown in
[dynamics-lift-2026-08-tasks.md](dynamics-lift-2026-08-tasks.md). Rows are `packages/**` →
engine-builder. Owner resequenced this pre-freeze 2026-08-14 (dynamics-lift-2026-08.md §7 decision
2; consumer-enablement-2026-08.md "Decisions taken" 1) — it no longer sits behind Arc OSS or Arc
PERF in `/start` order, which is why it leads this list. DYN-2 waits on the consumer-enablement
release reaching the restricted registry, not on the public flip.

- **DYN-2 — consumer swap (cross-repo, `grzott-portfolio`).** Delete the local module, repoint the
  one import, verify settle parity. Runs in the portfolio's own harness. Spec: §7.
- **DYN-3 — sleeping / resting-velocity damping (v2, deliberate).** Resting bodies settle in
  *position* but keep a small bounded velocity oscillation (~2.6 units/s in the package's test
  scenario) at the penetration-slop equilibrium — inherent to a Baumgarte-biased sequential-impulse
  solver run without sleeping. Sleeping thresholds + damping are what would let boxes fully come to
  rest; deliberately cut from v1 (§6). Spec: §6.

Goal: real, current, automated perf numbers + hard gates before the Oct 5 freeze. **Full specs in the SoT: [perf-measurement-2026-07.md](perf-measurement-2026-07.md)** — §1–§8 first-pass audit, §9–§10 second-pass verification (18-agent adversarial, 2026-07-28: render 3.1× regression re-confirmed live; secondary drift table withdrawn; B11–B26 new findings), **§11 row specs** — rows here are lean pointers (one-source-per-fact); the builder reads the row's §11 section before planning. Routing: PERF-3, PERF-9 and PERF-10 are the `packages/**` rows → **engine-builder** (one lockstep minor changeset each); all other rows touch benchmarks/`scripts/`/`.github/`/`apps/bench-*`/`docs/plans/` → **[MT] main-thread**. Sequencing below is the execution order; **PERF-1→5 are the pre-freeze must** (the §7 "defensible release" core + gates), PERF-8 is cheap and pre-freeze-preferred, PERF-6/7 slide post-flip if the calendar tightens (PERFORMANCE.md then declares web/device unmeasured, §8). Owner-OOB items (portfolio corrections, TestingBot account check, EAS bench project, engine-repo secrets) are ledgered per-row in §11, never blocking. Pick top → `/start`.

- **PERF-4b — bench coverage extension (P1 remainder) [MT].** New per-frame suites for animation / postfx / tilemap / pathfinding / math — contract entries, smoke rows, `innerIters` sized to the 0.3–25 ms window, workspace deps; ends with a 5-run re-capture + promote + `bench:md`. Requires PERF-4a. Spec: §11.4b.
- **PERF-15 — web lanes have no staleness guard (found 2026-08-19) [MT].** `check:perf-freshness`
  covers only the five node suites (`freshness.suites`), and nothing in `scripts/` or CI watches
  `web.json` / `web-gpu.json` / `web-android.json` at all — a web capture can go arbitrarily stale,
  or be deleted, with every gate still green. The node lane's own history is the argument: the
  freshness canary is what caught the instrument change that started this arc. Assert each lane's
  capture exists, carries `runs >= 2`, and names a commit reachable from HEAD; stale it on
  `render-web`/`render`/`postfx` src changes since that commit. Wire into the jobs already running
  `check:perf-freshness` (`ci.yml:52`, `release.yml:47`). Spec:
  [benchmarking-completion-2026-08.md](benchmarking-completion-2026-08.md) §3.
- **PERF-14b — the ~2x straddle survives the power-plan fix (found 2026-08-19) [MT].** PERF-14 is
  done (see `## Done`) and the correction was real, but the run log shows the bimodality is **not**
  fully explained by the power plan. Of the 11 captures taken at `f457e82` under High performance at
  0.97-1.92% foreign load, the first eight read `ecs.step 1000` 8.9-9.8 µs / `render.flush` 21.1-22.9
  µs / `integration.frame` 4.91-5.19 ms, and the last three — consecutive, on the same quiet machine
  — step to **16.4-16.5 µs / 44.9-45.5 µs / 6.29-6.54 ms**. A coherent ~1.8x mode change after eight
  back-to-back runs, which is the *opposite* of what the runbook's "run the five back-to-back with no
  pauses" instruction assumes. `bench:promote` would refuse a window straddling it (3 of 5 deviant),
  but a five-run window landing wholly inside the slow mode publishes silently — the original PERF-14
  hazard, still live. Candidate causes not yet separated: turbo/thermal budget exhaustion under
  sustained load, or the machine's known WHEA-corrected microcode instability (BIOS 1611, microcode
  0x11F — a BIOS update is scheduled). **Do this before trusting the next capture:** decide whether
  the protocol pauses between runs or asserts mode-coherence across the window, and record which.

- **PERF-7 — native bench app + device-lane revival (P3) [MT + owner OOB, 2 sessions].** `apps/bench-native` engine scenarios (sprite/rotation/postfx/particles + texture-eviction/resume-from-background + cold start); lift harness from **PT HEAD only** (absolute-ceiling gate) with the B20 fails-green hardening (matrix validation, staleness gate, zero-combo fails, absolute-delta floor, one scenario-id namespace, artifact-path fix); revive per B21 order — TestingBot account verification FIRST, not a fresh APK; weekly cron with window ≥4× cadence + per-release anchor; bridge device data to the dashboard (kills dead device.json — B22); rewrite the parked particle-storm gate below. Spec: §11.7.
- **PERF-9 — physics broadphase allocation + O(n²) update (B27) (engine-builder, `packages/**`, post-freeze).** `SpatialIndex.update`'s per-entity closure + O(n) find; `entries` destructuring in the broadphase loop; the reused pair-`Set` that costs ~38× the heap footprint of a fresh one under JSC (verify on Hermes FIRST — RN is the primary target). One lockstep minor changeset. Spec: §11.9.

### Arc HYG — post-flip engine hygiene (SOLID/DRY/KISS/YAGNI; NOT before the flip)

Design-quality cleanup from the 2026-07-05 audit ([ledger](_local/deep-audit-2026-07-05.md) §6; specs road-to-oss §17). Correctness-neutral — the correctness/API-freeze spillovers were pulled forward into B25–B31 + B30. `/start` exhausts Arc OSS first; these run after the flip. All verified duplication / dead code / leaky idiom, not speculative.

- **HYG1 — math: dedupe collision predicates + spring aliases.** `intersection.ts` free fns ≡ Rect/Circle namespace predicates → one source; `springIn/Out/InOut` are misnamed Back curves (no oscillation) → alias-or-rename + fix docstring (easings.ts:167-181).
- **HYG2 — ecs + core: dead param + honest docstring.** `Prefab.spawn(overrides)` — implement or drop the ignored param (prefab.ts:14/:34); `AppLifecycle` docstring → "manual adapter" (it has no RN AppState auto-pause/resume).
- **HYG3 — render + postfx: SkSL + effect de-duplication.** Six zone-bg apply-fns → one descriptor-table `applyZoneBg` + shared factory builder (frame-renderer.ts:548-678); shared `HASH*/VALUE_NOISE` SkSL snippet constants (shaders.ts, 9 copies); assemble COMPOSITE_SKSL from shared fragments (rain layers ≡ WEATHER_RAIN).
- **HYG4 — shapes: fill/stroke epilogue + color unpack.** Extract `fillStrokePath(...)` for the six shape renderers (draw.ts:196-474); one `argbToFloat4Into` color helper replacing the 5 copies (shapes/render/render-web/react/assets).
- **HYG5 — simulation + physics + input + audio: integrator + hot-path.** `nbody.step()` calls `verletStep` (makes api-specs:837 true); `SpatialIndex` Map-keyed O(1) update/remove; `pollGamepads` single Map get + `applyRemapInto`; drop `AudioManager.factory` dead field; `gestures` `_pinchActive` boolean.
- **HYG6 — scene + animation + pathfinding + dialogue: shared helpers.** `_teardownStack()` (dedupe ×3 + drop go()'s double-run); import `linear` from math + document chainTween-needs-a-TweenManager; internal `heap.ts` shared by astar/jps + drop `funnelSmooth` `_mesh`; make `ActionHandler` sync-or-awaited (kill the dead `Promise<void>`).
- **HYG7 — storage + debug + devtools + progression: dead writes + idiom.** Drop `__flare_doc_version`/`__flare_version` dead writes + dedupe `DocumentStore.load` first-run branch; shared `nowMs` + `StatsTracker`→factory; wire-or-drop `devtools.enabledOnNative` + shared TimeControl/TimeScrubber core; `resolveTier` via `resolveTierIndex`.
- **HYG8 — react + cli + iap + render-web: shared factories.** One subsystem-wiring factory behind `GameView`+`createGame`; share `parseTemplateArg/parsePmArg` + single template-id source; `getGrantsForProduct` via `getCatalogEntry` + single `ProductType`; `createWebSurface` returns a `{flush,dispose}` handle (or fold into C1's `createWebFrameRenderer`).

### Arc XPACK — engine extraction from PT + flare-engine-page proofs (APPROVED 2026-06-30)

Goal: lift proven-in-game, genre-agnostic logic into the engine. **Full detail + evidence + API sketches + risks per row live in the audit SoT: [extraction-audit-2026-06.md](extraction-audit-2026-06.md)** — todo rows stay lean pointers (one-source-per-fact). All rows route **engine-builder**, `packages/**` only, **lift-with-strip** (zero PT/shmup/IP coupling), zero-alloc hot paths, full gate green. Engine-first: anything PT still uses gets the engine package first, then PT swaps its local copy for the import. Sequencing below is the recommended order; pick top → `/start`.

Headline: the web render backend is **proven** (flare-engine-page runs core+math+particles+postfx-SkSL unmodified on a CanvasKit-WASM surface that already satisfies `render`'s `CanvasLike`). Three new packages fall out: `render-web`, `simulation`, `progression`.

- **XP1 — render-web parity spike (read-only, blocking gate for XP2). DONE (2026-06-30, #191) — functional GO; `CanvasLike` delta: none.** `SpriteBatch` calls no canvas methods (it hands commands to a `drawAtlas` callback owned by the bridge), so `CanvasLike` stays the `Renderer` transform contract and maps 1:1 to CanvasKit `Canvas`; XP2 must ship a web frame-renderer (CanvasKit analog of `createSkiaFrameRenderer`), not just `createWebSurface`. The one XP2 adapter gap = object-form (`SkRect[]`/`SkRSXform[]`) vs CanvasKit's flat `InputFlattened*Array` for `drawAtlas`; routes A1 (RN-Skia web bridge) / A2 (CanvasKit-direct shim, recommended). Perf is a later live-browser spike (out of scope here). Full resolution: [audit §1 XP1 resolution](extraction-audit-2026-06.md).
- **XP2 — `@flare-engine/render-web` package. DONE (2026-06-30, #192) — engine-first DRY refactor (owner-chosen over self-contained re-impl).** Lifted the backend-neutral frame-renderer out of `packages/react/src/skia-bridge.ts` into `packages/render/src/frame-renderer.ts` (`createFrameRenderer` + structural `FrameRendererModule`/`DrawSurfaceLike`/`PaintLike`/`RectLike`/`RSXformLike`/`PictureRecorderLike` — zero RN-Skia types in `render`); `react`'s `createSkiaFrameRenderer` now delegates to it (native behavior byte-identical, full react suite green). New `@flare-engine/render-web` (peer dep `canvaskit-wasm >=0.40.0`, not in the `engine` barrel): `loadCanvasKit(wasmUrl, onProgress?)` (injectable URL, fs fallback), `createWebSurface(kit, canvas): CanvasLike`, `createWebSkiaModule(kit)` (the A2 CanvasKit shim — object↔flat drawAtlas via grow-only Float32Array scratch, `_ckPaint` extraction, BlendMode enum map), `createWebFrameRenderer`, parameterized `createPostFxSurface(kit, canvas, chain?)`. Headless CPU-surface conformance test reads back real pixels (red sprite inside / black outside) — every per-sprite effect + zone-bg comes free from the shared frame-renderer. **Review caught + fixed 3:** shallow non-throw conformance test → real readPixels lock; `rotate` radians→degrees broke backend parity → pass-through to match native (radians/degrees quirk still a separate cross-backend cleanup); per-frame `Float32Array` alloc on stretch/zone-bg → pooled scratch. Gate green (lint 41 / typecheck 59 / test 79 / build 40). Full resolution: [audit §1](extraction-audit-2026-06.md). Unblocks the web `GameView` upstream (page spike). **Correction (2026-07-05 audit P13, resolved 2026-07-09): `createWebFrameRenderer` was NOT shipped by XP2 — OSS-C1 implemented + exported it from `packages/render-web/src` (2026-07-09, alongside `loadCanvasKit`/`createWebSurface`/`createWebSkiaModule`/`createPostFxSurface`), so the row's export claim now holds.**
- **XP3 — `ecs` movement + cull + follower (closes a real hole). DONE (2026-06-30, #193).** New `packages/ecs/src/systems.ts` ships 5 zero-alloc `SystemDef` factories: `createMovementSystem` (Position += Velocity·dt), `createLifetimeSystem(world, onExpire)`, `createBoundsCullSystem(world, bounds, onCull)`, `createFollowerSystem` (pins Position = target+offset, skips missing target), and the split-out `createOrphanCullSystem(world, onOrphan)`. New `Lifetime` + `Follower` components (appended after `Parent`, no bit-shift); new `CullBounds` type = plain readonly numbers (**no physics dep** — bounds passed in, destroy via caller callback, so ecs stays physics-free). All three cull systems use a closure-owned grow-only scratch (`.length = 0` reset) so destroy-in-callback can't skip entities under live query iteration. `Parent` left untouched (no consumer; `Follower` is the richer replacement). 6-agent adversarial verify (1 implement + 5 lenses + 1 fix) caught + fixed **1 major** — Biome line-width on a test import that `biome check src/` missed (the lint-gate-skips-tests trap; husky would've unstaged) — then hardened tests post-verify (bounds batch-destroy guard, inclusive-boundary `<`/`>` contract, orphan cross-step re-fire). README + api-specs updated. Gate green (80 ecs tests). Audit §4.
- **XP4 — `math` angle helpers (merge shortest-angle-diff + homing-steering — two agents found the same primitive). DONE (2026-06-30, #194).** Added flat `angleDelta(a, b)` (shortest signed `a − b`, branch form, wraps to `[-π, π]` — exact ±π pass through) + `rotateTowards(cur, target, maxStep)` to `scalar.ts`, and zero-alloc `Vec2.turnVelocityToward(vx, vy, dx, dy, maxStep, out)` (mandatory `out`) to the Vec2 namespace; exported from `index.ts`. **Byte-identical to PT `homing.ts:53-63`**: `Math.hypot` speed, inline `2 * Math.PI`, single-correction branch form (not modulo), `(a − b)` operand order, `cos/sin × speed`. New `tests/angle-parity.test.ts` = adversarial **ULP bit-sweep** vs 3 verbatim PT-reference fns (12-angle ±π boundary grid × speed/maxStep variants + 2000 `SeededRNG` samples/helper, compared with `Object.is` for bit-exactness). 6-agent adversarial verify caught + fixed **1 real blocker** the green gate hid: the random-sweep called `rng.float()` with no args → `NaN`, and `Object.is(NaN, NaN) === true` made ~6000 assertions pass **vacuously** (one verifier proved a deliberately-broken `angleDelta` got 0 mismatches); also a latent TS2554 in the test (the [[typecheck-gate-skips-tests]] trap) — both fixed by `rng.float()` → `rng.next()`. Post-verify polish: doc range `(-π,π]`→`[-π,π]`, removed a cancelling test literal. README + api-specs updated. Gate green (414 math tests) + root tsc clean. Audit §4.
- **XP5 — Berek gaps → `physics` + `tilemap` (second-genre proof). ENGINE HALF DONE (2026-07-01, #196); Berek re-wire + TPL1 = downstream.** New `packages/physics/src/solid-grid.ts`: `SolidTile` (structural `{row,col}` — physics stays **tilemap-free**; `TileCollider[]` is structurally assignable, so callers pass `extractColliders(map)` directly), `SolidGrid`, `buildSolidGrid`, `isBlockedAABB` (half-open `EDGE=1e-4`), zero-alloc `moveAndSlideInto(grid,x,y,dx,dy,hw,hh,out)` (axis-separated; y-check uses post-x position; `out===pos` aliasing safe via locals). All **byte-identical to Berek `src/movement.ts`** (only renames + `Into` out-param form + `readonly`), so Berek can later delete its local copies for a zero-change swap. `tilemap`: `TilemapRenderer.drawInto(camera, batch)` queues culled tiles into a caller-owned `SpriteBatch` without flushing (frame owner flushes once → tiles z-order under sprites); `draw(camera, flush)` kept, now delegates (behavior-identical). 6-agent adversarial verify: **parity + zero-alloc + layer-purity + root-tsc/biome-on-tests all pass**, 0 blocker/major; fixed 1 tautological test post-verify (`drawInto`-no-flush now asserts queue-without-consume via a real caller flush). README + api-specs updated. Gate green (physics 68 / tilemap 41 tests) + root tsc clean (no new test-file backlog). **Downstream (separate, not this session):** Berek swap (own repo/gate, needs engine consumable via 0.1.1 publish or workspace dev-link) + TPL1 topdown-template wire. Audit §4.
- **XP6 — `@flare-engine/simulation` scaffold + verbatim cores. DONE (2026-07-01, #197).** New package `packages/simulation` (40th dir), lifted from sibling **flare-engine-page** `demos/spacetime/`. **`integrate/nbody.ts`** + **`wave/wave-packet-pool.ts`** are **verbatim lifts** (code byte-identical — confirmed by zero-output diff; only page-demo header vocab stripped, incl. de-demo'ing two stray "lattice" words the first pass missed): `createNBodySystem` (velocity-Verlet, Plummer softening, COM/`absorb`/`closestPair`/`totalEnergy`, optional GR precession) + `createRipplePool` (analytic expanding wavefronts). **`integrate/verlet.ts`** is net-new (generalized from nbody's step): `AccelFn` + self-contained `leapfrogInto` (KDK, 2 evals/step) + cached-a(t) `verletStep` (1 eval/step), both zero-alloc/symplectic. **Reality check on deps:** the XP6 cores import **nothing**, so the package has **no runtime deps** (math/core join in XP7 with field/agent — the row's "deps math+core" was aspirational). Ported the real `nbody`/`ripples` tests (energy-conservation, ring-travel) + new verlet tests (harmonic-oscillator energy bounded to 1%/2000 steps — a **real symplectic guard**: forward-Euler drifts out of band by ~200 steps). 7-agent adversarial verify: lift-fidelity + verlet-symplectic + zero-alloc/wiring + realness all **pass**; the one "major" (flare-engine-page dirty tree) was a **false positive** — that's the owner's separate branding WIP, this tree has only `packages/simulation/` + 3 docs. ### Arc PT-EXTRACT — deep cleanup → move Pan Tvardowski to its own repo

Goal: PT becomes its own GitHub repo; flare-engine keeps only the engine packages + the `create-flare-app` CLI base game and references PT as the store showcase (3 free / 5 paid). **Cleanup first (in-monorepo, pure refactor, all 1413 tests stay green), then extract.** Engine consumption after split = **private/restricted npm packages** at `0.1.0` (owner buys paid npm plan; PT pulls `@flare-engine/*@^0.1.0` via authenticated `.npmrc`). Full plan + 7-agent audit (2026-06-06) at `~/.claude/plans/audit-deeply-ios-configuration-vectorized-gizmo.md`. All CL rows: route **game-builder**, zero `packages/**`, **no behavior change**, full gate green.

**Phase 1 — cleanup: COMPLETE.** CL6–CL11 landed (sessions #176–#181, all in `## Done`); every GOD file split, zero behavior change, full gate green throughout. Phase 2 is next.

**Phase 2 — extract. Session plan S1–S9 (supersedes the old SP1–SP5). Full detail + gap ledger: approved plan `~/.claude/plans/ok-big-day-iom-quizzical-matsumoto.md`.** Owner prereqs: paid npm plan + `@flare-engine` org + `NPM_TOKEN` (before S5); new GitHub repo + new EAS project (before S7); store listings + RevenueCat SKU (ship-time).

Prep arc — **COMPLETE** (S1–S4b landed #183–#187, all in `## Done`). The PT app is self-contained, folios retired, engine staged for restricted publish. **Everything below is owner-gated.**

Publish + extract — owner-OOB, gated:

- **S5 — Rehearsal: DONE (2026-06-08).** Caught a publish blocker — internal `workspace:*` deps would ship **broken** (`changeset version` doesn't refresh `bun.lock`'s workspace versions → bun publish pins stale `0.0.0`; and `@changesets/cli` 2.30 has no bun branch → `npm publish` leaves `workspace:*` literal). **Fixed:** `scripts/concretize-workspace-deps.ts` rewrites `workspace:*` → `^<version>` after `changeset version`, wired into the root `version` script (commit `5da397a`). Clean re-rehearsal of the real `bun run version` confirms `@flare-engine/engine` publishes all 15 internal deps at `^0.1.0`. See [[engine-publish-concretize]].
- **S6 — Real publish 0.1.0: DONE (2026-06-09).** All **36 scoped `@flare-engine/*` published at `0.1.0` restricted**; PT app stayed private (unpublished). Verified by a clean `bun add @flare-engine/engine@0.1.0` from the real registry in a throwaway dir → 15 internal deps resolved at `^0.1.0`, zero `workspace:`/`0.0.0` leak, transitive `math@0.1.0` on disk. Local flow: changeset (`changelog:false` transient to skip `GITHUB_TOKEN`) → `bun run version` (concretize) → commit `1f1bb5e` → `bun run release`. **Three publish prereqs learned (see [[engine-publish-prereqs]]):** (1) auth needs the right token type in the local npm config (specifics in the private ledger); (2) the **`@flare-engine` org** must be on a paid npm plan for private publish; (3) the root `release` script's `turbo --filter=./packages/*` glob fails under Bun's shell — fixed by single-quoting (`fix(release)`). CI auto-publish via **Trusted Publishing / OIDC** (no stored `NPM_TOKEN`) is the follow-up; flip `ENABLE_RELEASE=true` once wired.
- **S7 — Stand up PT repo (history-preserving split). IN PROGRESS (2026-06-10): Phase A DONE** (filter-repo split, 405 PT-only commits at root, `monorepo-archive` + `perf-baseline-pre-A` tags, pushed to github.com/grzott/Pan-Tvardowski; engine release tags stripped). **Phase B DONE** (standalone config committed; gate green standalone: typecheck 0 err / lint 406 files / 1469 tests / expo-doctor 17/19 — found+fixed: missing `@flare-engine/iap-revenuecat` dep, missing biome/ts/bun-types devDeps, missing real `.gitignore`, stdin Buffer cast in parse-boot-trace). **Phase C DONE** (fresh `.claude/` harness per ws337exio: Stop-hook 5-gate verify with green-state cache, jq bash guard, agents-may-commit, AGENTS.md 128 lines + CLAUDE.md `@AGENTS.md` shim; husky/commitlint/lint-staged; lean CI; `FLARE_DEV=1` metro dev-loop; lint widened to `scripts/` killing the [[lint-gate-skips-scripts]] gap; 12-agent adversarial verify confirmed 2 findings, both fixed — dangling `src/privacy/registry.ts` doc claim + missing `.husky/pre-commit` which is owner-created, classifier-blocked). Remaining owner setup: `.husky/pre-commit`, `NPM_TOKEN` secret on PT GitHub repo + EAS. Owner decision 2026-06-09 (supersedes the 2026-06-08 fresh-init call): **preserve PT's git history.** `git filter-repo --path apps/pan-tvardowski --path-rename apps/pan-tvardowski/:` extracts PT's commits with files rewritten to the repo root — clean because the path-confined builders kept commits partitioned (the split is by path, not commit-message scope; conventional scopes just verify it). Tag the last monorepo-era commit `monorepo-archive` (or the first standalone commit `v0.1.0`) as the old↔new boundary milestone. flare-engine keeps its **own** full history (S8 `git rm`s the app files; the commits stay in the log) — so **both** repos carry history. Dramatically lighter than the monorepo harness: `AGENTS.md` (<150 lines; `src/visual-spec/` = SoT) + `CLAUDE.md` = `@AGENTS.md` shim (CC reads CLAUDE.md, not AGENTS.md); `settings.json` (permission allowlist + **Stop hook** + narrow PreToolUse git guard) + gitignored `settings.local.json`; `hooks/verify.sh` = typecheck→lint→test + balance + locale (no build/scope/SESSION_LOG), wired as the Stop gate; KEEP balance-freeze; `outputStyle: Terse`. DROP: both builders, commit-block dance, SESSION_LOG, build gate, blanket git-mutation ban, post-edit-lint. Add skills only when a flow repeats.
- **S8 — Slim flare-engine: DONE (2026-06-10).** `apps/pan-tvardowski` removed (moved to `../_pt-extracted-archive`; owner deletes at leisure — the standalone repo + git history hold everything). Full gate green after removal (lint 39 / typecheck 56 / test 75 / build 38 turbo tasks). Deviations from the plan row, both verified: (1) workspaces **keep `apps/*`** — `apps/bench-dashboard` (benchmarks viewer) stays; (2) **`perf-bench.yml` deleted**, not kept — `bench-cloud.sh` hard-requires `apps/pan-tvardowski/maestro/` + `.env.testingbot`, so the nightly can never run here again (root `scripts/perf/` + retired folios kept as archive per plan). Also: README/CONTRIBUTING/docs/skills/agents de-PT'd (PT = external showcase link), stale folio-SoT claim in `roadmap.md` fixed → `src/visual-spec/`, dead `build-plan.md`/`pan-tvardowski-build-plan.md` links fixed, root `eas-build-post-install` dropped, workflows bumped `checkout@v5` (Node-24 forced 2026-06-16). Deferred: 8 package READMEs still say `apps/pan-tvardowski` — cosmetic, published at 0.1.0; sweep them at the next engine docs pass / 0.1.1.
- **S9 — Cross-repo compat guard + nightly bench re-home: DONE (2026-06-10), owner config pending.** 5-agent audit drove the design (run wf_44b210d7). **(a) Compat guard lives in the PT repo** (`compat.yml`, nightly 03:00 + manual) — NOT the engine repo: zero new tokens vs a cross-repo PAT, and post-publish dispatch races npm propagation. Core primitive (validated live, bun 1.3.11): `bun update --no-save $(grep bun.lock names)` — frozen-lockfile CI can NEVER catch engine drift, names must come from `bun.lock` not package.json (core/debug/input/scene are transitive-only), `--no-save` leaves package.json+lockfile byte-identical. Caret ceiling documented: engine minors freeze the guard → PT range bump is on the release checklist (CONTRIBUTING). **(b) Perf bench re-homed** (`perf-bench.yml` ported flat, `contents:write` commit-back, PR-label trigger on PT-local `src/fx/**`+`src/lighting/**`; engine `packages/postfx/**` PR-time coverage is lost by design — caught at next nightly). ## Deferred (needs a balance pass, not a refactor)

_The PT row below is kept as the written-up conclusion of an investigation run here; the app itself moved
to [grzott/Pan-Tvardowski](https://github.com/grzott/Pan-Tvardowski) on 2026-06-10, so its file
references are paths in **that** repo, not this one, and the work is tracked there._

- **PT — WEAPON_DEFS + BULLET_DEFS damage to runtime.** Investigated session #136, **reverted, deferred**. The row's premise was false: the old `WEAPON_PROJECTILE_SPECS` spec table in `config/gameplay.ts` (`damagePerBullet`/`cooldownMs`) was **unwired dead data** with zero combat consumers — confirmed and deleted in CL1 (#171). The actual shipped balance is hardcoded: every player bullet does `damage: 1` (`src/spawn.ts:227`, applied at `src/systems/collision.ts:41` `hp.current -= bullet.damage`), and fire cadence is `ship.fireRate = 0.18` (`src/spawn.ts:62`), never reset from any spec. `WEAPON_DEFS` values (damage 10/18/25/22, iskry cooldown 120ms) match that removed spec table, not live play — so wiring them to the runtime is a **~10× damage + ~1.5× fire-rate buff**, not a provably-equivalent refactor. There is no zero-drift path that also makes `WEAPON_DEFS` a meaningful source of truth (the only zero-drift authoring is all-damage=1 / iskry-cooldown=180, which makes the table trivial). Reopen only as part of an explicit balance/retune pass with enemy-HP rescaling and on-device playtest — out of scope for the visual-spec arc.

## Future tracks

### Perf — particle-storm optimization (conditional, rescoped 2026-06-01)

The old Tier A→D plan is **dead**: every tier targeted the shadow-rim stack, which was cut in #152 (its gates `shadowRebuildMs.p95` / `rebuildShadowTexture` reference deleted code that can never be measured again). E8 baseline (2026-05-26, **n=1 per cell**) shows nothing is over budget — postfx p95 ~0.03 ms (free), and the worst frame-p95 cell is **particle-storm: 9.52 ms Pixel 10 / 6.69 ms Pixel 6 / 3.67 ms Galaxy A55**, all comfortably under the 16.67 ms budget. So there is no bottleneck to chase today.

- **P1 — (conditional) particle-storm optimization.** Route: TBD by the finding (likely engine `packages/render` batching + the PT spawn/`Lifetime`-tick loop, now cross-repo). **Gate to start:** the nightly rolling 7-day median accumulates ≥7 samples/cell and confirms particle-storm p95 is trending toward budget on the mid-gate, OR a regression is flagged. **The nightly was deleted with `perf-bench.yml` in S8 (it required the in-repo PT app) — the gate is parked until S9(b) recreates the bench in the PT repo.** Until that median exists (still n=1), no perf work is warranted. Scope only the path the `particle-storm` scenario stresses (ECS `Lifetime`-entity tick + draw), not a shader chain. Supersedes memory `perf-optimization-plan` (now stale).

- **PERF-13 — repeat-and-fold for all three web lanes** (2026-08-18). Every published web figure
  was a **single run** until now. New `benchmarks/src/web-promote.ts` (pure, 16 tests) folds N
  captures per lane into a median-of-medians with the observed spread band, driven by
  `bun run bench:web:promote -- --lane <cpu-raster|gpu|android> --runs N`. Fail-closed like the node
  lane: refuses a window whose captures disagree on commit / runtime / browser / renderer / phone,
  refuses a fold of one (its band would falsely read zero), refuses a drifted scenario set, and —
  on device — refuses a capture taken while Android reported **thermal throttling**, read directly
  from `dumpsys thermalservice` either side of every run rather than inferred. An **unreported**
  thermal status is refused too: "unrecorded" is not "not throttling". **Thresholds calibrated
  against 15 healthy captures (5 per lane), not chosen:** largest single-run deviation from the
  folded median was CPU-raster 7.9% / desktop GPU 8.0% / Android 4.3%, so the 10% ceiling has only
  ~1.25x magnitude headroom — what carries the gate is that it takes **two** deviant runs, and
  across all 15 healthy captures no scenario had even one. Notably the **desktop lanes are noisier
  than the phone**, the opposite of the intuition that motivated a device guard. Battery
  temperature is recorded (23.4 -> 25.7 C across the folded device window) but **deliberately not
  gated** — no throttled capture has ever been measured with this instrument, so any drift
  threshold would be invented; the same from-below-only limit PERF-11 recorded. All three lanes
  re-captured 5x and folded; doc + dashboard gained a Spread column and a Runs count, with the
  band fields **required** in the validator so an unfolded capture cannot render
  (mutation-verified). **Measured while doing it:** the Bun teardown panic is not the one-off the
  first pass recorded — 4/5 on CPU-raster, 1/7 on Android, 0/5 on GPU, so the exit code is not a
  reliable success signal on the long lane; README corrected.

- **On-device Android web lane (`bench:web:android`)** (2026-08-18). Real phone numbers, built on
  the corrected sampler rather than before it. `apps/bench-web/scripts/android-driver.mjs` +
  `runAndroidMode` in `run.ts`: the bench server stays on the host, `adb reverse tcp:<port>` makes
  `localhost:<port>` on the phone resolve back to it, and the driver **attaches** to the phone's
  Chrome through its `@chrome_devtools_remote` socket via `adb forward` + `connectOverCDP`.
  `playwright._android`'s `launchBrowser()` was tried first and **hangs indefinitely** against
  retail Chrome — the attach path is the one that works (probed end to end before any driver was
  written). The browser belongs to the phone's owner, so only the page this driver opened is
  closed; Chrome keeps its pid, verified. Provenance is read from the hardware via `getprop`
  (`ro.product.model` / `ro.product.device` / `ro.soc.model` / `ro.build.version.release`) and
  recorded per row, so a capture names the phone it ran on — which mattered immediately: the device
  on the desk is a **Pixel 7** (panther, Tensor GS201, Mali-G710), not the Pixel 9 it was expected
  to be. Same software-rasterizer refusal as the desktop GPU lane. Own label
  (`runtime: "web-android-gpu"`) and own files (`web-android.json` / `web-android-history.ndjson`),
  never merged into a desktop series. **First capture: 0/18 rows quantized at either 60 Hz or
  90 Hz**, confirming the sampler fix holds on device. The phone costs **1.2x-2.0x** the desktop's
  submission time on flush scenarios but only **1.1x-1.3x** on `render.sort` — pure JS with no
  surface, isolating single-thread throughput, where the Tensor lands near a desktop i9.
  `parseAdbDevices` split out and tested (7 cases) because the states that are *not* usable —
  `unauthorized`, `offline`, `no permissions` — otherwise look like a ready phone and fail
  mid-capture; the lane refuses zero devices and refuses to guess between several. Exporting from
  `run.ts` exposed a module-level `main()` that ran a whole capture on import, now behind
  `import.meta.main`. **Known flake recorded, not papered over:** Bun panicked with
  `integer overflow` during teardown on the first run (once in two), *after* every result file was
  written; it is a Bun bug by its own crash message, and the README says to check for the files
  before assuming a capture was lost. **Dashboard wired the same session:** all three web lanes now render at
  `/web`, `web-gpu.json` and `web-android.json` gained package exports, and the validator's runtime
  check became **per-lane** rather than global — each lane asserts only its own label, so a
  `web-gpu` row cannot render inside the CPU-raster table even though that label is legitimate
  elsewhere (mutation-verified: relabelling one android row fails the build naming the lane). The
  three identical tables collapsed into one `WebRenderTable`, which is what makes the `Ops/batch`
  column present on every lane instead of one. **Still not done:** repeat-and-fold — no
  `bench:promote` equivalent, so a device row is one sample from a warm phone — and no
  thermal/governor control. Row **PERF-13** below.

- **Web sampler: vsync quantization fixed + both lanes re-captured** (2026-08-18). Found while
  scoping the Pixel lane. `sampleRafDeltas` took the `requestAnimationFrame` frame-to-frame delta
  as its sample and divided by `innerIters`; rAF is vsync-locked, so every published web figure was
  `k x 16.667 ms / innerIters` for integer `k` — **all 36 rows across both lanes**, with **13 of 18
  GPU rows at exactly k = 1**, reporting the display refresh rate and nothing about the engine.
  Work cheaper than one refresh interval could not be represented at all. New
  `apps/bench-web/src/sampler.ts` brackets the work with `performance.now()` and uses rAF only to
  schedule, growing each batch to a ~20 ms window (dominating Chrome's 100 us Spectre clamp) rather
  than a fixed `innerIters` sized for the node bench — which also self-calibrates across
  CPU-raster / GPU / mobile, the property the Pixel lane needs. Fail-closed: a batch whose clock
  never advances is **refused by name**, never published as zero. 8 new tests are known-answer
  oracles (synthetic work of exactly known cost, recovered to 6 decimals) plus a quantization
  regression; **mutation-verified** — reverting to frame-delta timing makes `k - round(k)` read
  exactly 0 and fails 3 of them. `ScenarioResult`/history gain `opsPerBatch`/`batchMs`/
  `frameDeltaMs`, **required** in the dashboard validator so a pre-fix capture cannot render
  (mutation-verified: dropping the field fails the build, naming row and field). **Numbers moved a
  lot:** GPU `render.flush 1000 / 1 atlas` 0.642 → **0.333 ms**, `render.sort 500 (0 swaps)`
  0.017 → **0.001 ms** (17x), CPU-raster `render.sort 500 (0 swaps)` 0.017 → **0.002 ms**; the
  headline CPU-raster-vs-GPU ratio moves 41x → **87x**. The GPU lane is also **relabelled as
  CPU-side submission cost** — `GrDirectContext.flush()` submits without waiting, so a clock
  bracket cannot see GPU execution, and claiming "GPU frame cost" was wrong independent of the
  quantization. `benchmarks.md`'s claim that the three `render.sort` rows read identically across
  lanes "because they touch neither surface" was **wrong about the mechanism** — both lanes had
  pinned them to the floor; corrected in place. Published in `ed579be`/`f030217` and already
  propagated to the portfolio's fact ledger — flagged for CE-W8.

- **PERF-10 — hot-loop `__DEV__` guard (B28)** (2026-07-29, session #266). Arc-PERF, engine-builder scope, one lockstep minor changeset (core + render). **The engine no longer evaluates an unresolvable global once per sprite.** JSC has no inline cache for an unresolvable global identifier, so `typeof __DEV__ !== 'undefined' && __DEV__` cost ~270 ns *per evaluation* wherever the flag is undeclared — and PERF-3 had put that expression inside `fillXforms`, i.e. once per sprite per transform pass, **nine times over under an outline effect**, while `Pool.release()` had carried it per released object since before this arc. Both now read `globalThis.__DEV__` as a property (~0.46 ns, ~400× cheaper, identically togglable at runtime). **Measured before and after on the PERF-4a bench**, `render.flush 1000 / 1 atlas`: undeclared **412–450 µs → 56–88 µs**, which is the same band the declared-`false` configuration occupies — the two are no longer distinguishable, where before it was ~7.4×. The 56/88 split is bimodal on this machine and shows up in **both** configurations (one interleaved pair came out reversed), so it is JIT/thermal state, not residual cost. **The published table cannot move and now says so** — the bench pins `__DEV__ = false`, so it always measured the cheap path; the win belongs to web/CanvasKit, Node and Bun consumers, and RN (which always defines the flag) is unaffected. **Deviation from the spec:** guards stay truthiness checks, not `=== true` — `=== true` would silently disable the pool's double-release guard for anyone who set `__DEV__ = 1`, and truthiness matches the old expression for every value, keeping the rewrite behaviour-preserving at identical cost. `Pool`'s **constructor** keeps the identifier form on purpose (once per pool; the site a bundler can fold). Render's dist canary was **inverted** — it used to assert `typeof __DEV__` survives tsdown, it now asserts the bare form never returns — and core gained a matching `pool-dist.test.ts`; both mutation-verified (reverting the source produces 6 failures). **Follow-up fix to PERF-5, found by using it:** `check:perf-freshness` compared committed history only, so at `/close` it reported FRESH for the very edits about to invalidate the capture and the red would surface only in CI after the push — fails-green at the last cheap moment to act. It now counts uncommitted changes to measured paths as staleness (`scripts/lib/git.mjs`, shared with check-perf.mjs); this row is the proof, correctly reding all five suites. Baseline re-captured (5 runs, clean tree) as the row's second commit. B28 closed. **PERF-8 next** (dashboard hardening + root `PERFORMANCE.md`), then PERF-4b and PERF-9 post-freeze.
- **PERF-8 — publish: dashboard hardening + PERFORMANCE.md** (2026-07-30, session #270). Arc-PERF P5 [MT]. **The dashboard can no longer fail green, and the repo now states what it does not measure.** New `app/lib/schema.ts` validates every field and THROWS — replacing `JSON.parse(...) as T` plus a headline lookup that returned `null`, so schema drift used to render `NaN` and a renamed scenario silently deleted a quarter of the overview page while CI stayed green (B25). This is a static export, so a throw is a **failed build**, not a broken page served to a reader. **Six mutations verified against real builds:** schema-version drift, renamed headline row, heap row missing its resolution, and `DEPLOY=1` on a 210-day-old capture all refuse; the same stale capture without `DEPLOY` still builds, and a fresh one with `DEPLOY=1` passes. **That pass caught `DEPLOY=1` being silently inert** — turbo strict env mode strips any variable a task does not declare, the identical trap that made `FLARE_BENCH_ALLOW_STALE` unreachable in PERF-1; the assert was written, looked right, and did nothing. Scoped turbo task now declares it and lists `benchmarks/results/*.json` as inputs, so a cache hit cannot redeploy stale HTML over a fresh capture. **Staleness banner on every page** (date, commit, runs, `__DEV__`, machine, foreign load, age — amber >14d, red >30d); the device page gets a permanent red banner because its data is **unrefreshable**, not merely old (B22). **Heap table reworked** to the PERF-4a schema — the old red/green bar sized by raw delta coloured GC noise as an allocation regression (B2); it now renders `< ±N B` under the instrument's resolution and shows convergence. **Deploy target decided as NONE, deliberately** — the recorded target (GH Pages beside `apps/site` via `next export`) was infrastructure that never existed; the quotable numbers live in `benchmarks.md` and `PERFORMANCE.md` behind CI gates, and adding an ungated publishing surface five weeks before freeze would add the problem this arc removes. **Owner may overrule — judgement call, not a technical constraint.** New root **`PERFORMANCE.md`** leads with what is NOT measured, iOS first (never measured on any iOS device, ever), then web/CanvasKit, the unrefreshable Android capture, eleven packages with per-frame hot paths and zero coverage, and no cold-start/memory/GC/multi-core numbers anywhere; three first-draft claims were corrected against the data before shipping. First arc row needing **no re-capture** — it touches no measured source. B25 closed, B22 addressed. **Arc PERF pre-freeze work is complete.**
- **PERF-11 — capture-time load guard (B29)** (2026-07-29, session #268). Arc-PERF [MT]. **The harness can now tell a quiet capture from a contended one — which it could not, on the day it published a headline row 23% low.** New `benchmarks/src/load.ts` measures **foreign CPU load** (system-wide utilisation from `os.cpus()` minus this process's own share from `process.cpuUsage()`) across the timed suites only — the window excludes the heap probes because they fork, and `process.cpuUsage()` cannot see a child, so spanning them would misreport our own work as a neighbour. **`promote` gained two independent refusals because neither subsumes the other:** foreign load >15% catches the *cause* but is blind to a resident process that pollutes cache without burning CPU; a run-to-run deviation check catches the *symptom* whatever the cause but is blind to a window that is internally tight at the wrong level — B29 produced exactly one capture of each kind. **The deviation check counts runs >10% from the FOLDED MEDIAN, not the min-max band**, a correction forced by the guard failing on healthy data the first time it ran for real: the band version refused a `particles.update 1000 emit+integrate` window whose five runs read 16.42/20.20/16.51/16.46/16.49 µs, when 13 of that row's 15 recorded runs sit inside 16.4-16.75. A median of medians absorbs one outlier in five by construction, so the gate must too; two deviant runs mean the median is straddling two states. Replayed over every recorded window — refuses B29's capture and the noisy `0b88096` PERF-2-era one, accepts every clean window. A run with **no** load record is refused too: "unrecorded" is not "quiet". **Both thresholds calibrated against measurement, not chosen** — foreign load on this machine in ordinary working state reads 4.36-5.43% (the realistic floor is ~5%, not zero), so the ceiling is ~3× that; **Stated limit:** the load ceiling is calibrated from below only — the machine state that caused B29 no longer exists and was never measured with this instrument, so only the spread threshold is validated against the real event, pinned in the tests by its actual recorded medians. The published doc carries the load figure beside the CPU model and states both ceilings. The first draft of this row asserted "undisturbed captures read well under 1%" — measurement said 4.5%, and the docstring was corrected rather than the number. B29 closed; 70 bench tests. **PERF-8 next.**
- **PERF-5 — Track A gate + freshness canary + freeze-gate homing** (2026-07-29, session #265). Arc-PERF P2 [MT]. **The perf claims are now enforced by something that runs without being asked.** Two CLIs over a shared pure core (`scripts/lib/perf-gate.mjs`, no I/O — every decision testable): **`check:perf`** reconciles the 11 exact-integer draw-path scenarios against a populated `.perf-baseline.json`, fail-closed in both directions (an improvement is still a change to a published claim and must be committed), with a liveness floor rejecting all-zero rows — a row of zeroes cannot tell "the hot path allocated nothing" from "the hot path never ran"; **`check:perf-bytes`** weighs all 41 published tarballs against ~10% ceilings, reusing audit-tarballs' `npm pack` + unbuilt-dist guard (both now in `scripts/lib/pack.mjs` rather than duplicated); **`check:perf-freshness`** (B24 keystone) asks whether the numbers still describe HEAD. **The canary compares reachability, not dates** — `git log <capture-sha>..HEAD -- <measured paths>`, with each suite's DIRECT imports expanded through the workspace dep graph at run time, so a `math` commit correctly stales every `render` row and nobody maintains that edge by hand; `benchmarks/src` stales everything (PERF-4a moved the headline row 486 µs→55.6 µs with zero engine changes). Absolute age stays where it already lived (`bench:md`'s 30-day refusal) rather than becoming a second copy of one policy number. **Rubber-stamp guard:** a change touching both `.perf-baseline.json` and a package's `src` must add a dated `reviews` line ≥20 chars. **Mutation-verified, not read** — 14 planted defects each produce the intended red, and that exercise found a real bug: `git status --porcelain` marks an unstaged edit with a leading SPACE, so trimming the whole output ate the first path's first character and the guard saw `perf-baseline.json`, blind to the one file it watches (fixed: untrimmed split + `-uall`). **Wiring:** ci.yml + release.yml get all three (B19 — a red CI does not stop release.yml, they are independent jobs), verify.sh gets the counter half on engine/mixed scope; `check:perf-bytes` is CI/release-only, since 69 s of packing on every local `/close` is how a gate gets commented out. **Freeze gate homed** in road-to-oss §11/H1 as the single source — flip-runbook and manual-test-protocol carry one reference checkbox each, no restated items — plus the Oct 5–18 cron-pause rule. Track B plumbing **decided** (§11.5b) for PERF-7: `perf-data` branch over `[skip ci]` on main (a nightly bot push would attempt a nightly release once `ENABLE_RELEASE` is true), local scheduled runner over self-hosted (fork-PR code post-flip), and the warn tier **pushes a pinned GitHub issue** — B22 is the proof that a dashboard nobody opens is not a signal. B7/B8/B19/B24 closed. Gate green. **PERF-10 next** (hot-loop `__DEV__` guard, engine-builder) — pulled ahead of PERF-8: three mechanical sites, cannot move a published number, and it is the difference between web consumers eating a ~270 ns-per-sprite deopt at the October flip or not.
- **PERF-4a — microbench honesty (P1 rework)** (2026-07-29, session #264). Arc-PERF P1 [MT], the first half of the split PERF-4. **The bench now measures the transform path it always claimed to.** New `skia-stub.ts` drives the real `createFrameRenderer` with a counting, zero-alloc Skia module (real setters — a no-op setter lets JSC delete the transform math as dead); render matrix widened to 18 rows covering interleaved *and* clustered atlas order, rotation fractions, alpha runs, and the tint/outline/stretch/zone-bg paths. In-process heap-delta replaced by an isolated subprocess probe that warms to the allocation plateau and reports its own resolution; **B26 withdrawn** — the "+15.7–33.4 KB/frame retained growth" was a 30-frame warm-up artifact, re-measured at ~0 with every structural counter flat. Real findings spawned instead: **B27** (physics pair-`Set` reuse costs 38x the footprint it saves under JSC → PERF-9) and **B28** (`typeof __DEV__` on an undeclared global costs ~270 ns/eval, sitting per-sprite in `fillXforms` → PERF-10). New exact-integer alloc gate (11 rows, runs in CI via turbo test) + schema v2 with fail-closed promotion (mixed schema, undeclared `__DEV__`, non-converged heap, disagreeing counters all refuse). A 35-agent adversarial review caught 2 blockers before commit — the `__DEV__` inflation (headline row read 486 µs instead of 57 µs) and a heap probe that could not converge at 2000 entities. Baseline re-captured (5 runs, commit `b7f5fc7`, all rows converged): headline `render.flush 1000 / 1 atlas` **55.6 µs**, `integration.frame 1000` **12.47 ms**, `ecs.step 1000` **23.3 µs** — **not comparable to any pre-2026-07-29 capture**, the instrument changed underneath. `benchmarks/tests/baseline.test.ts` now runs the `--markdown` guards under `bun test` so a drifted baseline fails CI instead of waiting for a human to type the command. Next: **PERF-5**.
- **PERF-3 — engine perf-observability counters** (2026-07-29, session #263). Arc-PERF, the arc's only `packages/**` row — engine-builder, one lockstep minor changeset (core/render/particles). **Every §4 Track-A counter now has an engine surface**, so PERF-4's bench can assert integers instead of inferring from wall-clock. **`Pool.created`** ([pool.ts](../../packages/core/src/pool.ts)) — the `acquire()`/`tryAcquire()` ternaries split to `if`/`else` so only the factory branch counts; **`prewarm()` deliberately excluded**, because the counter exists to detect steady-state allocation and pre-filling the free list is setup, not a frame cost. **`ParticlePool.created`** forwards it — the ParticlePool's `Pool` is private and not injectable, so without this forward its allocation is unobservable from a bench. **`SpriteBatch.allocCount`** increments only past the pool-hit early return, so a hit costs nothing. **`resetXformCounters()`/`getXformCounters()`** — two module-level ints in `fillXforms` behind the proven `typeof __DEV__ !== 'undefined' && __DEV__` runtime guard ([[tsc_parse_error_poison]]-adjacent precedent: pool.ts:37-39 survives to dist); reset is unguarded so a bench can call it in any build. **The counters count transform WRITES, not sprites** — `drawOutlined` runs `fillXforms` 9× per run (8 offset passes + 1 main), and all 9 count. The adversarial pass proposed suppressing the 8 offset passes so a 1-sprite outlined run reads `1`; that was applied and then **reverted on review**: §4 asks these to prove "transform work is linear, not quadratic" and "the cheap path is actually taken", and hiding 8 of 9 real `cos`/`sin` computations from a transform-work counter defeats both — in an arc whose premise is perf honesty. Documented instead, exactly parallel to how `drawCalls` was handled. **`stats.drawCalls` ≡ `batchCount` aliasing resolved by documentation, not repointing** — repointing at real canvas calls needs a count threaded back through `FrameRenderer.drawAtlas` → `SpriteBatch.flush`, coupling two decoupled modules; the JSDoc + api-specs now state drawCalls = flush-RUN count and name the real fan-out envelope (9 `drawAtlas` for outline, n `drawImageRect` for stretch — where outline degrades to alpha-only — n `drawRect` for zone-bg), and the equality is scoped to "whenever `SpriteBatch.flush` is the only writer" because `recordDrawCall()`/`recordBatch()` are independent public methods and the package's own `renderer.test.ts:80-84` already asserts a diverged state. **Dist canary** (`frame-renderer-dist.test.ts`) greps BOTH `dist/index.js` and `dist/index.mjs` for the literal `typeof __DEV__` (the ESM artifact is what Metro and every web bundler actually load via the `import` condition, so a CJS-only grep would miss a per-format `define`) and asserts no `typeof true`/`typeof false` substitution artifact. **11-agent Workflow** (4 disjoint-file implementers → docs/changeset → 5 adversarial lenses → fix; wf_138d39e0-a0b): 16 findings, 4 blocker/major. **The blocker was the dist canary itself** — its first form self-built math/events/assets/ecs/render in `beforeAll`, and tsdown's `clean:true` empties those `dist/` dirs for ~72% of a build while turbo runs sibling `test` tasks at concurrency 10, so `bun run test` went red with a random-victim `Cannot find module '@flare-engine/*'` (reproduced 5/5 forced, victims across testing/engine/particles/create-flare-app) **and then self-healed on retry via turbo cache** — an intermittent red with no relation to any defect. Removed entirely: `turbo.json` already declares `test.dependsOn:["build"]` → `^build`, so dists are guaranteed fresh before the suite runs, and the [[typecheck_gate_skips_tests]]-era assumption that "verify.sh runs test before build" is false for the gate path. Mutation-verified the new tests (moving `_allocCount++` above the pool-hit return, making `prewarm` count, dropping a `__DEV__` guard all fail the intended test); one particles test was found **vacuous** — it never drained the prewarmed free list, so it passed with `release()` deleted — and was rewritten to drain first. **Bookkeeping:** minor changeset (never major → forces 1.0.0, [[oss_changeset_bump_lockstep]]); no user-facing strings; new `render` exports needed no `packages/react/tests/{game-view,safe-area}` mock-stub update (verified — `game-view.tsx` reads neither). Gate green (lint/typecheck/test/build FULL TURBO/deps/changeset "40 changed packages, all covered"); render 213 tests. SESSION_LOG #263 canonical. **PERF-3 done; PERF-4 (microbench honesty — drive `createFrameRenderer(countingStubModule)` so `fillXforms` actually executes) is next and consumes these counters.**
- **PERF-2 — correct the record** (2026-07-28, session #261, commit `eec7129`). Arc-PERF P0 [MT]. **The repo no longer publishes April's numbers.** Captured **5 independent runs first, before writing any code**, so all five carry `gitDirty:false` at `0b88096` — a baseline that cannot name the commit it measured is the disease this arc treats. **New `bench:promote`** (`benchmarks/src/promote.ts`, pure + 10 tests): folds the last N history runs into one baseline as **per-scenario median-of-medians** plus the **run-to-run spread band**. Per-scenario matters — the median *run* for `ecs.step` is not the median run for `render.flush`, so promoting one whole run publishes another scenario's outlier as fact. **Fail-closed by design:** refuses runs that disagree on `cpuModel`/`gitSha`/`runtime`/`platform`, refuses any run captured from a dirty tree, refuses contract-drifted or shape-mismatched runs, refuses a damaged (partially-appended) history log — the output becomes the reference every later run is judged against, so averaging-anyway is the wrong default. `opsPerSec` is **recomputed** from the aggregated median rather than aggregated (a median-of-rates is not the reciprocal of the median-of-times, and the row would contradict itself). Schema gained optional `bandMinNs`/`bandMaxNs`, `runs`, `aggregatedFrom`; `generated` is the newest source capture, **never** the promote time. **Numbers (median of 5, spread):** `render.flush 1000/1` **89.4 µs (76.0–124.1)** vs April's single-run 26.6 µs = **3.36×**; `ecs.step 1000` control **23.3 µs (23.2–25.9)** vs 23.7 = flat, confirming code not machine; `integration.frame 1000+300p` **12.00 ms (11.95–12.73)** vs 9.01. **The spread column is the real deliverable** — `render.flush` swings 76–124 µs between identical runs, so every single-run number this repo ever published was a coin flip inside that band; it also settles an audit dispute (a one-off rerun had claimed physics got *faster* at 0.88×, but 5 quiet runs put it at 2.76 ms vs April's 2.09 ms — neither single measurement was trustworthy). **Generator:** `<!-- do not edit -->` header (the file is machine-owned and silently overwritten), `_Captured:_` under **every** table so a copied table carries its own date, Spread column, and a **"What these numbers do not measure"** section stating plainly that per-sprite transform cost is excluded (B1 — the flush bench never constructs the frame renderer) and that multi-atlas rows are worst-case run-breaking rather than batching (B17); device rows stale-labelled in place with the dead capture pipeline named (B22). **READMEs:** `particles` no longer claims "Zero per-frame GC during gameplay" — the pool provably recycles, but nothing in the repo can currently measure GC, so it now states what's provable and points at the dated benchmarks; `physics` gained a measured-cost pointer. **Bookkeeping:** `history.ndjson` **deliberately tracked** (the append-only record every baseline derives from — a promoted number stays traceable to its runs) with `--no-record` as the local escape, documented; turbo `test` `inputs` narrowed so a capture no longer busts the test cache; dead code removed (`toMarkdownRow`, `reportToGroups`, `findResult`). No changeset (READMEs only, no `packages/*/src` — [[changeset_gate_src_only]]). Gate green; root tsc unchanged at the 9 pre-existing TH2 errors. The per-package typecheck extended by PERF-1 caught a loose type in the new test — the blind spot closing itself. SESSION_LOG #261 canonical. A 9-agent adversarial review was still in flight at commit time; findings land against PERF-3 or as a follow-up. **PERF-3 (engine perf-observability counters) is next — the first `packages/**` row in the arc, routes engine-builder, needs a lockstep minor changeset.**
- **PERF-1 — bench harness automation-hardening + dead-fork delete** (2026-07-28, session #259). Arc-PERF P0-prereq [MT]. Made the off-device bench stack safe to run unattended. **B11 (bench executed by no gate):** new `benchmarks/tests/smoke.test.ts` runs every suite at `samples:2/innerIters:1/warmup:0` via a new `BenchOverrides` param threaded through all 5 suite factories, asserting a frozen scenario-name contract — wired by a `test` script + `"@flare-engine/benchmarks#test": {dependsOn:["^build"]}` override (the global `test` task depends on the package's own `build`, which benchmarks lacks, so without the override it would have run against stale `dist`). Verified in the real task graph: resolves to `bun test` behind `ecs|math|particles|physics|render#build`; 10 tests / 1.2 s, so it rides CI + verify.sh at negligible cost. **Proven non-vacuous by mutation** (`ecs.step`→`ecs.tick` broke 3 tests). **B12 (every run overwrote the tracked baseline):** runs now append `results/history.ndjson`; `microbench.json` moves only behind `--write-baseline` — proven by a full 2.5-min run leaving it byte-identical. **B13 (forged freshness):** `--from-cache` retired; `--markdown` renders ONLY the committed baseline with an honest `Captured:` vs `Rendered:` split + a 30-day staleness refusal (`FLARE_BENCH_ALLOW_STALE=1` to override — the env form survives the bun→turbo boundary that swallows `--flags`). **B14:** provenance (cpuModel/cpuCount/totalMemMB/gitSha/gitDirty) on every capture; **hostname deliberately excluded** (repo flips public); legacy baselines render `CPU: unrecorded` rather than a plausible default; the dirty probe is scoped to `packages benchmarks/src` so the bench's own output can't self-poison the flag. **B15:** shell `>` into the tracked doc replaced by in-script temp+rename (unique-pid temp, `finally` cleanup) — also applied to the baseline write. **B18:** three scoped turbo bench tasks force `^build`. **B9:** hard-deleted `scripts/perf/` (5) + `docs/perf/` (3) — a fork that couldn't run even manually (`bench-cloud.sh:46,78` targeted the long-gone `apps/pan-tvardowski`) and whose 32 ndjson rows are a byte-identical prefix of PT's 74; PT owns the living harness. **12-agent adversarial-review Workflow** (5 lenses → 6 refuters → critic; wf_18e2c0d4-261): all 6 blocker/major refuted on spec-literalism, but 4 mechanisms held up and were fixed on merit — baseline written non-atomically (the row's own exit criterion named it), `gitDirty` self-poisoning, `--allow-stale` unreachable through `bun run`, and tautological test assertions (one lens measured a 35% escape rate for `median > 0` on a gutted bench) → replaced with a shared `findContractViolations` now guarding BOTH the live suites and the published baseline, so a doc can no longer be emitted with a headline row silently missing (verified: exit 1, drift named, nothing written). **Bookkeeping:** no changeset (no `packages/*/src` — [[changeset_gate_src_only]]); no user-facing strings; no render/react export. Generator hygiene relocated the hardcoded 2026-04-22 gate decision to `docs/design/native-acceleration.md` and killed a dead `build-plan.md` link the generator would have reintroduced. Gate green (lint/typecheck/test/build FULL TURBO/deps/changeset); root tsc unchanged at the 9 pre-existing TH2 errors. SESSION_LOG #259 canonical. **Follow-up (session #260):** the review's completeness critic landed after the row closed and found a real blocker — turbo runs tasks in **strict env mode**, so `FLARE_BENCH_ALLOW_STALE` was stripped before the script saw it and turbo's arg parser rejects `-- --allow-stale`, leaving `bun run bench:md` with **no working path** while the baseline was stale. Fixed by declaring `"env": ["FLARE_BENCH_ALLOW_STALE"]` on the task + a PowerShell-form hint in the error and README ([[powershell_commit_block_env]]). **PERF-1 done; PERF-2 (correct the record — 5× capture + honest benchmarks.md) is next.**

Source-based (no build needed — matches the spec's "generated project referencing workspace paths"); `strict:true` only (consumer snippets aren't held to the engine's `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`/`verbatimModuleSyntax`); `lib` explicit ES2022+DOM (the engine is Web+RN — render-web/audio/input/share snippets legitimately use DOM types). Diagnostics map back to `README.md:<line>` for the fixer; per-package PASS/FAIL/no-snippets report + inventory; `check:readme` wired in root `package.json`; generated dir gitignored (mirrors the `nodenext-probe` precedent). ` ```ts no-check ` opt-out for intentionally partial fragments. **Poison-masking bug found + fixed during build:** one snippet with a parse error makes tsc skip SEMANTIC checking for the ENTIRE program (all-or-nothing — verified: a single broken `.tsx` arrow masked all 137 real errors, harness falsely showed 40/41 green). Fixed with a **liveness canary** — a file with a deliberate SEMANTIC error (TS2322): if the canary's error is absent from a run, the semantic pass didn't run → the harness drops the snippet(s) tsc flagged and re-runs, and if the poison isn't attributable to a snippet (a parse error in workspace SOURCE reached via the paths map, a bad tsc invocation, or truncated output) it **ABORTS LOUD (exit 2)** rather than greenwash. tsc is the sole syntax authority → no parser-divergence false-fails. **Adversarial review** (20-agent Workflow: 4 lenses — parsing/path-map/orchestration/result-integrity — × per-finding refute) caught the **major FALSE-PASS class** the initial design still had: a parse error in a workspace-source file (or any tsc run producing zero snippet-attributed diagnostics — global option error, tsc launch failure, `maxBuffer` truncation) was swallowed by `runTsc`'s catch → 0 errors → all PASS exit 0; the canary + generic-diagnostic scan closes it (verified via isolated repro: foreign source parse error → canary absent + no snippet poisoner → exit 2). Also folded in: dropped the Bun.Transpiler pre-gate (it can false-FAIL valid TS — the [[biome_typeof_import_bun_parse]] divergence), broadened the fence regex to the `typescript` alias case-insensitively (a differently-tagged fence would silently green the package), stopped remapping a missing SUBPATH export onto index.ts (honest TS2307 instead of a masked pass), removed a dead `startLine` field. DOM-lib Window-global false-pass (`name`/`length`/`close`/…) confirmed **zero current instances** and documented as an accepted gap (dropping DOM would false-FAIL legit web snippets). **Result:** 9/41 green (a11y, analytics, cli, create-flare-app, engine, iap-revenuecat, leaderboards, progression, simulation), 32 failing, 62 snippets checked, **137 errors** inventoried for D2–D4 — incl. real API-truth bugs (`SeededRNG.float()` needs 2 args; `loadCanvasKit()` needs `wasmUrl`; storage's phantom `react-native-mmkv` peer). Done-when satisfied: green on already-correct packages, red on known-bad, inventory produced. **Bookkeeping:** **no changeset** — D1 changes no shipped package `src`/manifest (only root tooling + a plan doc; root pkg is `private`), so `check:changeset` is a structural no-op ([[changeset_gate_src_only]]); a changeset here would wrongly bump all 40 packages for a root-only change. No user-facing strings (no localization cascade). No render/react export added/renamed → no `game-view`/`safe-area` mock-stub rule. Gate green (lint `biome check .` incl. the new script / typecheck / test / build FULL TURBO / deps / changeset). SESSION_LOG #247 canonical.

All **41** `packages/*/package.json` + root `package.json` + `bun.lock` + `.changeset/oss-c2-packaging-manifest-polish.md`. Implemented directly on the main thread via a deterministic transform script (the 41 exports blocks were byte-uniform — audited `41/41` before editing) + targeted edits for the one-offs; **no `src` touched**. **engines:** `{"node":">=20"}` added to the **39** manifests that lacked it (cli + create-flare-app already declared it), inserted before `publishConfig`, matching the existing floor. **Exports — Form-A per-condition types (decision + why):** each `exports["."]` moved from flat `{types, import:str, require:str}` to `{import:{types:"./dist/index.d.mts",default:".mjs"}, require:{types:"./dist/index.d.ts",default:".js"}}`, and the `"."`-level `"types"` was **dropped** (root `main`/`module`/`types` kept as the node10 fallback). Rationale: TypeScript matches the `types` export condition **first**, so a retained `"."`-level `types` would **shadow** `import.types` and re-introduce the exact `.d.ts`-served-to-ESM masquerade attw flags — Form-A (no `"."`-types) is the only shape where an ESM consumer actually resolves `.d.mts`. tsup already emits both `index.d.mts` **and** `index.d.ts` per package (`format:['esm','cjs'], dts:true`), so **no build change** — the `.d.mts` targets are real. **attw proof (the done-when's "or prove attw-clean"):** `@arethetypeswrong/cli --pack` on math/core/react/engine/ui-shop/cli → **"No problems found 🌟"** across node10 / node16-CJS / node16-ESM / bundler; a disk sweep confirmed **287/287** referenced export targets exist and **zero** residual `"."`-level `types`. **@types/react `^18.0.0`→`^19.0.0`** in exactly the **10** named devDeps (achievements, analytics, devtools, errors, haptics, iap, leaderboards, react, ui, ui-shop) — aligns the declared range with the `react >=19` peers + the root `overrides` `^19.2.0` (already the installed version). **Root `maxrects-packer` devDep dropped** — grep-verified used only by `@flare-engine/cli`, which carries its own dep + `import` (root copy was dead); `bun install` removed exactly 1 package, `bun.lock` reconciled clean (no attw bunx pollution). **engine umbrella description** `"…re-exports all packages"` → `"…re-exporting the core runtime and React packages"` (it re-exports 15 core deps, not all 40). **Drift correction (the row's stale peer item):** the spec's *"add the dropped `react-native-safe-area-context` peer"* was **already satisfied** — **C1** (#236) added it to `engine` (peer present at `:55`), and the engine peer set is accurate as declared, so C2 made **no peer change**; the struck-through ui-shop RN-lowering (SUPERSEDED by C1) was correctly left untouched. **Adversarial review:** a 4-dimension Workflow (spec-completeness / exports-resolution / version-floors / consistency-drift) × per-finding refute pass, each dimension running the real tools (attw, `changeset status`, manifest scans) → **0 findings, 0 confirmed**. **Bookkeeping:** ONE changeset lists all **40** scoped `@flare-engine/*` pkgs at **minor** (create-flare-app excluded — private), fixed-lockstep → **0.3.0, never major** ([[oss_changeset_bump_lockstep]]; `changeset status` confirmed patch:0 / minor:41 / **major:0**). `check:changeset` is a structural no-op for this row (it counts `src/**` only; C2 touches none) — the changeset is added anyway to keep the 0.3.0 CHANGELOG honest about the consumer-visible packaging change. A `(kit,canvas)` version would have to own present+flush (which `createWebSurface` deliberately hides) — the spec's own "not truly 1:1 → defer" case; kit-only ships a real, correct factory now. **Owner approved at the plan stop.** New `web-frame-renderer.ts` + `index.ts` export; api-specs entry authored; conformance test extended to drive one frame through the factory (beginFrame→drawAtlas→endFrame→drawPicture→readPixels, discriminating non-black pixel); the XP2 todo "Correction (P13)" line updated to "shipped in C1". **postfx-surface resize fix (§16, C1-owned):** `resize()` recreated scene+pong but never the on-screen surface (`onscreen` was `const`; `MakeWebGLCanvasSurface` binds the canvas backing store at creation, so a resized canvas left a stale-size onscreen target). Fix: `let onscreen`; `resize()` now re-`MakeWebGLCanvasSurface(canvas)`, deletes the old surface on success, keeps the previous + warns on null (onscreen stays non-null → all closures safe); B31's `blit()` LUT-child binding untouched; dispose still deletes the current onscreen. New postfx-surface test asserts onscreen is recreated on resize (fails on pre-fix). **C1b validation — both "Done when" fixtures proven STRUCTURALLY** (deterministic, resolver-independent, stronger than a lockfile snapshot; scratchpad script): (1) topdown template on Expo57/RN0.86/React19.2/RN-Skia2.6.x — every bumped peer range satisfied across the full 16-pkg closure → **0 version conflicts** (only unmet peers are `react-native-audio-api`, a pre-existing template gap for B14, not a C1 range change); (2) web-only render+render-web+assets runtime closure (assets, ecs, events, math, postfx, render, render-web) has **0 RN/react hard deps + 0 required RN peers** → **no RN tree** (the page repo's install pain, fixed at source). Corrected in both the C2 todo pointer and road-to-oss §17 C2. **Adversarial verify** (6 review dims × per-finding refuters): 7 findings, **2 confirmed** (both minor, changeset **prose** only — the skia narrative omitted the postfx bump; safe-area-context mislabeled "optional" when it's a required engine peer) → both fixed; the manifest agent's prose "9 react pkgs" was a self-miscount (the tree correctly has 11 — verified by direct manifest re-read, not prose). **Bookkeeping:** ONE changeset — 16 peer-changed pkgs + render-web, all **minor** (fixed-lockstep → 0.3.0, never major — [[oss_changeset_bump_lockstep]]; `bunx changeset status` confirmed no major); `check:changeset` OK (30 covered). No engine-barrel change (render-web unbarrelled); no react/render export added/renamed → **no `game-view`/`safe-area` mock-stub rule**; no user-facing strings → no localization cascade; render-web→render/postfx are legal downward edges ([[render_web_l4_and_dep_guardrail]], `check:deps` OK). **Friction:** biome would have failed the collapsed `console.warn` line width in `postfx-surface.ts` (src is gated but the agent's self-check missed the wrap) — re-wrapped, one gate re-run. Gate green (lint/typecheck/test/build FULL TURBO 42/deps/changeset) + root tsc clean. SESSION_LOG #236 canonical.
- **OSS-B31 — latent web/devtools correctness: render-web LUT child binding + devtools resume timescale** (2026-07-08, session #235). Thirtieth Arc-OSS row (engine; P2 — no shipping consumer today). `packages/render-web/src/postfx-surface.ts` + `packages/render-web/tests/postfx-surface.test.ts` + `packages/devtools/src/time-scrubber.ts` + `packages/devtools/tests/time-scrubber.test.ts` + `packages/postfx/src/runtime.ts` (doc-only) + `.changeset/oss-b31-web-devtools-correctness.md`. Preceded by a **5-agent deep audit** (Part-1 fix-correctness, Part-1 testability/docs/C1-overlap, Part-2 devtools, gate/blast-radius, adversarial refuter) — both bugs **CONFIRMED real code defects but PURELY LATENT** (the refuter verified nothing wires `color-grading-lut` into a web chain and nothing pairs `TimeScrubber` with slow-mo), matching the P2 "no shipping consumer" label; framed as latent-gap closure, not live-bug fixes. **Three spec corrections.** (1) **Stale path:** the "no extra children" doc is `packages/postfx/src/runtime.ts:74-82`, NOT the spec's `render-web/src/runtime.ts:80` (that file does not exist — wrong package AND line). (2) **Done-when STANDS, no B24-style amendment:** B24's "CPU CanvasKit can't rasterize RuntimeEffect" was specific to `drawAtlas`; the postfx `blit()` path uses `drawRect`+shader-paint, which the audit empirically verified rasterizes on CPU (incl. a two-child LUT) — so "reads back graded pixels" is CI-testable and is tested for real. (3) **SkSL order verified:** `uniform shader src`(0) → `uniform shader lut`(1), so `[srcShader, lutShader]` is correctly ordered. **Part 1 — render-web `blit()` (postfx-surface.ts:114-141):** `blit` bound only `[srcShader]` and never appended `pass.children`, so `color-grading-lut`'s LUT sampler was unbound on the CanvasKit web backend (`makeShaderWithChildren` would get 1 child where the SkSL declares 2). Fix: bind `[srcShader, ...pass.children]`, each `pass.children` **SkImage** wrapped via `makeShaderOptions` into a Shader (raw images can't be passed — mirrors the native `<ImageShader>` wrap documented in `skia-bridge.ts:48-52`). **Zero-alloc** (AGENTS.md hot-path): the reused `childrenBuf` is resized **in place** (`childrenBuf.length = 1 + pass.children.length`, no `new`); non-LUT passes stay length-1 (no-op). **Critical caveat both p1-fix + refuter flagged:** `childrenBuf` is reused across passes/frames, so it MUST be re-sized + null-slotted per pass or a stale LUT child leaks into a later/non-LUT pass and breaks it — the fix nulls all slots after each pass. Each wrapped child shader is `.delete()`d after `drawRect` (GPU-handle leak otherwise); the underlying LUT `SkImage` is caller-owned and never deleted. Plus fixed the stale postfx `runtime.ts:74-82` `CompiledPass.children` JSDoc ("no extra children today" → `color-grading-lut` yields one, the LUT at index 0 of the extras / shader index 1). **Part 2 — devtools `TimeScrubber.resume()` (time-scrubber.ts:57-61):** `resume()` hardcoded `timescale=1`, clobbering an active slow-mo. Fix: `_savedTimescale` field (default 1); `pause()` saves the current timescale **guarded by `!== 0`**; `resume()` restores it. The guard is essential — `rewind()` calls `pause()` internally, so an unguarded save would let a double-pause store `0` and **freeze the loop on resume** (refuter's regression trap; the old hardcoded-1 was accidentally immune). Mirrors `@flare-engine/debug`'s `TimeControl`; the shared pause/step-core dedupe stays **out of scope → HYG7**. **Tests** (all proven to fail on pre-fix src via HEAD-revert): render-web +3 — mock-spy "LUT pass binds `[srcShader, lutShader]` and frees the wrapped shader" (`childrenCounts [1]→[2]`, discriminating), "non-LUT effects still bind exactly one child" (regression guard against stale-child leak, `[1,1]`), and a **real-CPU pixel readback** driving `COLOR_GRADING_LUT_SKSL` with an all-blue LUT over a red source → read-back pixel is blue (5 expect() calls confirmed executed, not skip-guarded); devtools +3 — slow-mo `0.5→pause→resume→0.5`, double-pause-no-freeze, `rewind→resume` returns to `0.5`. **C1 overlap:** `createWebFrameRenderer` (C1, P0) does not exist yet; it composes the sprite/frame pipeline over a web surface and owns the `postfx-surface.ts:184` **resize** fix — a DIFFERENT region from `blit()` (114-141), so no semantic conflict. B31 landed first; C1 must rebase onto B31's `blit()` edit, NOT revert it, and is NOT superseded by it (a sprite renderer, not a postfx path). **Bookkeeping:** ONE changeset — render-web **minor** + devtools **minor**. **Postfx deliberately excluded** (doc-only comment; coverage already satisfied by the pending b30 changeset; a postfx changelog line would falsely advertise a behavior-neutral change — one-source-per-fact). No localization; no react mock-stub rule; no new tier edge (render-web→postfx already declared; render_web_l4 guardrail intact). Pre-gate friction ([[lint_gate_skips_scripts]]): biome would have failed the collapsed `makeShaderWithChildren` call's line width in src — re-wrapped before the gate. Gate green (lint/typecheck/test/build FULL TURBO 42/deps/changeset) + root tsc clean on all touched files. SESSION_LOG #235 canonical.
